## Configured admission factory and resource lifecycle — integrated

The missing factory/host/liveness/resource contract and later cancellation/physical-thread-exit clarifications were reported in [state/data/API](STATE-DATA-API.md#admission-factory-liveness-and-local-resource-ownership--implementation-contract) before their implementation/repair. One typed coordinator, ordinary-worker runner and local resource owner now serve the configured factory. Beta host configuration requires explicit admission bounds/reference. Ordinary startup reads original enrollment; absence/corruption/mismatch fails startup and a held gate stays held. Exact GET/HEAD /health/live returns static non-data liveness without downstream access; readiness remains denied. Previous-runtime active entries hold ordinary work even after expiry; only original confirmed completion can release bookkeeping. Normal shutdown closes clients after join; cancellation/timeout leaves failed status and uses pre-registered physical/local exit notifications plus an explicit cleanup request. A processing-finally callback alone cannot authorize client closure. No privacy proof, force-clear or owner deletion authority follows from an empty local owner.

## Durable admission coordinator and explicit runtime adapters — prior integration

The private coordinator stores one exact OPEN/HELD gate and bounded active-work registry. Original intents cannot renew deadlines on retry; gate conditions fence racing admission. Expired/crashed/unconfirmed work remains active until actual completion/reconciliation, so restart and TTL cannot establish drain. Exact gate/registry/runtime references are rechecked before a caller can use a private drain proof as conditional-commit inputs. It grants no owner permission and offers no general resume/force-release. Enrollment references are external approval identifiers, not authenticated approval by this adapter.

Explicit ASGI wrapping registers the whole awaited request/background scope, copies/resets context, checks response fragments and disables file offload that could outlive registration. Unknown cancellation retains work. The optional ordinary-maintenance runner registers before runtime/job/quota/authority access and preserves existing service roles/budgets. Failed release prevents that runner starting more work. Async functions and deferred awaitables/futures cannot be reported as completed synchronous work. Existing unconfigured driver behavior is retained; factory wiring and all independent entrypoints remain required.

The [candidate audit](../evidence/local/metadata-admission-candidate-verification.json) verifies **400 pure source / 400 installed-package cases**, 1,200 phases each, and **431 source-native / 431 installed cases**, 1,293 phases each. Counts overlap: 76 new current unit cases, 120 archived original cases, 204 adjacent pure cases, eight new actual-store cases and 23 existing maintenance cases. New native paths exercise authenticated /me blocking, conditional capacity/races/lost replies/restart/expiry and the real erasure executor. S3 is intercepted and identities/images are synthetic. The [canonical audit](../evidence/local/metadata-admission-integrated-verification.json) passes those same 431 cases on transferred source. ## Reproducible local checks

Dated implementation/checkpoint passages below are historical records of their then-tested source. Their counts and statements about matching files do not describe later changes. The current scope table and latest source-bound receipt own current status; a link to verification.json always resolves the latest run, not an older checkpoint. Original failures and archived receipts remain evidence, not obsolete draft specifications.

The local test-runner harness now collects the entire Python suite and runs whole-module cohorts (target 250 tests) against fresh owned local databases, with a 900-second guard per cohort. Total full-suite elapsed time can exceed the former single-process guard. Exact combined node and setup/call/teardown accounting rejects omission, duplicate, skip, failure or interrupted execution. The old timed-out batch remains preserved. This changes neither the five-second worker-startup, 750 ms worker-call, capture freshness nor lease controls; every product limit remains enforced independently.

The local verifier also checks the immutable synthetic corpus and runs all 144 development findings. The real worker-to-verifier sequence demonstrates complete → current → removed part → no current result with controlled clocks and a test durable-evidence fixture; it does not prove a real camera, session, S3 commit or AWS transaction. Windows tests exercise actual OS allocation denial, per-call deadline termination and explicit worker replacement requirements. Full Txx/E evidence remains unaccepted.

1. Python 3.12: `python -m venv .venv`, then `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pip install -r requirements-dev.txt`. Install Java 17+ and run `.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/dynamodb_local.py --install` once to fetch/verify the pinned local emulator. The runtime is cached under `.runtime`; no AWS account credentials are needed. Windows dependency pins/hashes are local platform evidence; generate and test a separate ARM lock before deployment.
2. Node: `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, then `npm run build` at the root. npm integrity hashes are in the root lockfile.
3. Run `.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/verify_local.py` for unit/contract/ASGI tests, local database lifecycle/concurrency, actual OpenCV proof, fixture/evaluation checks, dependency consistency, Python/frontend builds and documentation validation. It starts a checksum-verified owned emulator on a fresh port with dummy keys and telemetry disabled, then terminates it and removes only its newly created test directory. Standalone access tests may use `PROOFPACK_TEST_DYNAMO_URL`, restricted to an explicit loopback endpoint; production code does not use that variable.
4. Run `scripts/verify_local.py --browser` with locally installed Chrome to start an owned Uvicorn process on a fresh loopback port, verify its exact built index/default readiness, run Chrome checks and terminate only that process. `scripts/browser_local.py` records this identity/cleanup. Manual preview may use `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m uvicorn proofpack.app:app --app-dir services/api --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000`. The API serves `apps/web/dist` in the checkout. Installed-wheel assets and the host command now have local verification; actual private configuration, installation/service management and target acceptance remain unverified.

Windows sandbox restrictions can block Python's local event-loop socket pair. The ASGI test diagnostics preserve that stack; the same checks run outside that restriction, without live AWS calls. Do not weaken or skip the assertions. Full workflow Txx acceptance and E01–E16 stay unaccepted; component coverage is recorded separately in [local verification](../evidence/local/verification.json). That receipt binds checks to source/artifact hashes, not Git commits (this repository has no commits yet).

## Failures found and repaired

- The first unit run found Pillow PNG verification failing after EXIF access (66 passed, one failed). Preserved original output/JUnit: [failure log](../evidence/local/unit-tests-initial-failure.log). Verification now precedes EXIF inspection on a separate stream. The original decoder test passes with malformed/size/format denials retained.
- TypeScript caught a missing declaration for CSS side-effect imports. Added Vite's client type reference; [original compiler failure](../evidence/local/frontend-build-initial-failure.log) is retained. Production build passes without weakening type checks.
- Review tightened dataset hashes to include grouping, permission and expiry, and denies non-finite auth clocks. Regression tests exercise these boundaries.
- Browser screenshot output is now explicitly rooted in the project evidence directory. The starting page's keyboard skip target and transport-failure retry are checked separately from functional inspection journeys.
- Browser input/click timeouts occurred intermittently with concurrent browser execution. Waiting for React's mounted state alone was insufficient. Original traces/logs and the failed full-run receipt are preserved. Serializing workers and activating the page explicitly passed all nine checks across three repetitions (two product scenarios and one independent static-document diagnostic per repetition); original keyboard, focus and pointer-click assertions remain. This supports the configured local harness, not a universal diagnosis of Chrome behavior or broader usability claim.
- Verification subprocess timeouts now terminate only the runner's own process tree and use log files rather than inherited output pipes. Tests preserve nonzero child exit codes and exercise a real bounded timeout. The interrupted earlier verification receipt remains a separate record.
- Successful-publication tests reproduced a stored pass being returned after its supporting observation pointer was removed. The status reader now derives INVALIDATED with zero lease when required evidence is absent. [Original regression failure](../evidence/local/publication-boundaries-first.log) and JUnit are retained; the same missing-pointer oracle remains in the suite. Receipt validation also rejected malformed keys but previously attempted storage for malformed content types/digests; it now validates those fields before any request. [Original adapter failures](../evidence/local/object-validation-first.log) and the [35-test adapter result](../evidence/local/object-validation.log) are preserved. Neither fix relaxes a deadline or evidence predicate.
- Multipart integration exposed a final-read freshness gap: authentication could finish after the lease expired while status still returned its earlier pass calculation. The [original failing probe](../evidence/local/lease-last-auth-first.log) is retained. Effective status now computes the remaining lease after its final blocking authentication read; the upload response also finishes metadata authorization before deriving status. The same regression passes in the [eight-case upload/freshness batch](../evidence/local/upload-http.log). Other checks cover actual local image processing, body-bound atomic retry, no processing on pending replay, originating-session isolation, expired permit denial, and no nonce consumption on parser/authority failure. Parser/dispatch tests cover bounded chunks, fields/headers/types/bytes, receive deadline and cancellation ticket ownership. These remain local ASGI/CV/database tests with intercepted storage, not hosted browser or real AWS evidence.
- The first operator browser batch used Playwright label matching that included select-option text and timed out before selection. Role/name combobox selectors fixed the test harness; [initial log](../evidence/local/operator-browser-first.log) and [traces](../evidence/local/operator-browser-first-traces.zip) remain. The fixture context's initial nominal-coordinate assertion invented a 0.05-pixel bound, which did not match enrolled subpixel marker corners. It now checks all four expected corners in pixel units against the synthetic profile's stated one-pixel envelope; [original failure](../evidence/local/capture-context-first.log) is retained. This does not relax calibration or physical accuracy gates. The current context checks deny owner-only/wrong-session and changed/missing dependencies; browser tests use intercepted APIs and canvas streams, with no authentication bypass added to the application.
- The first full operator run reproduced a duplicate-admission interleaving: an old inspection snapshot followed by the now-consumed permit returned Denied before the commit-conflict history lookup. [Original full receipt](../evidence/local/operator-full-first-verification.json), [failure log](../evidence/local/operator-admission-race-first.log) and JUnit are retained. Admission now performs one bounded exact-operation history lookup on conflict/denial, revalidating current operator/source session before returning a reference. It never readmits, reprocesses or verifies changed state. Deterministic forced-read tests cover the exact-body winner and changed-body rejection; the existing concurrent one-consumption oracle remains. [Targeted regression](../evidence/local/operator-admission-regression.log) passed. Frontend checks also cover explicit occupied-station handoff, stopping on failed actions, fresh grants before camera permission and retiring late grants after Stop.
- The first full publication batch recorded 471 passing tests and one fixture setup error from a real worker exceeding the unchanged five-second startup bound. Preparation denied service; [original full receipt](../evidence/local/publication-full-first-verification.json), log and JUnit are preserved. The fixture now guarantees registry/client cleanup even when preparation fails. A separate spawn-safe [startup diagnostic](../evidence/local/worker-startup-probe.json) measured 2202/500/515 ms for three fresh workers, which does not explain or erase the prior timeout or qualify loaded-target performance. An earlier stdin-based diagnostic was invalid on Windows spawn and is explicitly excluded from product evidence. Use the latest full receipt for the subsequent gate result.
- An access API test found that a valid restricted invitee received 401 for a forbidden member-only route. The dependency now distinguishes invalid credentials (401) from valid but insufficient scope (403); no additional access was granted. [Original failure](../evidence/local/access-lifecycle-scope-failure.log) and its JUnit result are retained. Cross-identity invitation objects use the contract's 404 rather than revealing existence.
- RSA tests found that the first key validator rejected valid `key_ops: [verify]` metadata. The verifier now accepts precisely that optional operation and rejects signing/other operations. [Original RSA failure](../evidence/local/cognito-first.log) and the focused [82-test regression](../evidence/local/cognito-regression.log) are retained; the full suite includes additional denial/deadline cases. The first connected test batch also exposed three test-contract mistakes (provider subject versus application ID, a keyword-only argument and raw database Decimal versus public integer version). Corrected those fixtures/calls with the same access-denial assertions; [original connected failure](../evidence/local/cognito-completion-first-failure.log), JUnit and failed verification receipt remain separate evidence. These fixture defects are not represented as production exploits.
- Adding the specified GET login endpoint made the previous POST-login rejection return 405 rather than the old absent-route 404. Updated the obsolete status oracle and retained explicit proof that the forged subject/MFA body creates no authenticated session. Unsupported methods now use the safe error envelope and retain their Allow header. [Original route-batch failure](../evidence/local/signin-http-first-failure.log) and JUnit are preserved. Logout additionally clears the pending OAuth cookie; a delayed browser callback without it cannot exchange a code or reopen the session.
- Implementing the owner runtime endpoint changed its unconfigured response from absent-route 404 to the contract's generic dependency 503. Updated that obsolete assertion while explicitly checking that neither runtime epoch nor artifact hash is exposed. The configured endpoint still requires an enabled owner with recent MFA. [Original runtime-batch failure](../evidence/local/runtime-control-first-failure.log) and JUnit are preserved; no anonymous runtime access was granted.
- Strict cursor syntax caused an existing `!`-tamper probe to return the documented malformed-input rejection rather than an authorization rejection. The regression now verifies malformed syntax separately from a well-formed forged signature; both remain rejected, and actor/view/expiry checks are retained. [Original cursor-batch failure](../evidence/local/recipe-cursor-first-failure.log) and JUnit are preserved. No cursor validation or authorization assertion was removed to obtain a pass.
- A focused station-status race reproduced CREATED remaining visible when the originating operator logged out between separate dependency snapshots. No pass was produced, but the status failed its authority-invalidation contract. The reader now takes a final TransactGetItems snapshot of the complete collected actor/inspection/station/runtime/data/configuration/reference set and requires every row to match before deriving status, then rechecks time and caller authorization. The same logout-between-reads oracle must return INVALIDATED. [Original focused failure](../evidence/local/station-status-snapshot-first-failure.log) and JUnit are preserved.
- Four initial S3 protocol tests checked the wrapper's `StreamingBody.closed` property. Inspection of the pinned Botocore implementation confirmed that its close method closes the underlying response stream instead. The tests now retain and assert that actual stream's closure on successful and failed reads; checksum/size/version/encryption rejection assertions remain. [Original SDK test failure](../evidence/local/evidence-objects-first.log) is retained. These were test-oracle errors, not reproduced S3 resource leaks.
- A new preparation test expected a state conflict when retrying a failed inspection, but the advanced station fence correctly caused an authority denial first. The regression now requires that denial and still verifies no worker restart, no budget reset and no bypass of reviewed closure. [Original preparation-batch failure](../evidence/local/observation-prepare-first-failure.log) and JUnit are retained.

## Access implementation boundaries

The configured initial owner subject and approval receipt must match a recent MFA-qualified provider proof before bootstrap, which is not exposed over HTTP. No first visitor claims ownership. No raw cookie, CSRF token or invitation capability is persisted; invitation replay derives its capability under an explicit server key. All ordinary writes recheck session/access revisions in their transaction; duplicate concurrent invitation requests resolve to the committed idempotency result.

Invitations reserve pending-plus-enabled capacity, expire/revoke/decline once, bind both provider subject and verified email, and cannot activate a member without acknowledging the configured notice version/hash. Optional reuse defaults off. Acceptance revokes the restricted session and requires a new provider sign-in; it never upgrades the old cookie. Existing disabled identities use access recovery, not a new invitation. Ownership transfer requires explicit target acceptance with recent MFA; role changes and session revocation advance durable grants so old cookies cannot revive.

These are local component/API results. `IdentityProof` is an internal trusted-adapter input. Access tests inject synthetic proofs after that boundary; separate adapter/completion tests verify locally generated RSA signatures, claims and protocol fixtures before session creation. Neither establishes an actual Cognito deployment, human MFA or consent. Programmatic runtime composition now exists as described below; actual host configuration/invocation remains incomplete and no real operator has been enrolled or emailed. Actual notices, identity retention/key management, provider policy, network expiry behavior, target quotas and recovery remain release dependencies. Current control-plane metadata endpoints do not enable inspections.

The human contract lacked owner invitation listing/version reads and an explicit decline route despite W03/T32 requiring these operations. Those scoped endpoints and a notice retrieval route now exist; the state/API contract was updated before treating them as implemented. Notices and authentication lifetimes remain owner-configured development settings, not externally approved policy.

The OAuth contract now specifies durable one-use consumption before any provider exchange, binding to a separate browser cookie, configuration/key rotation invalidation, and a post-transaction deadline check. `OAuthFlows` persists only hashed state/browser capabilities and derives verifier/nonce under the private deployment key. Separate service instances can read the same pending/consumed flow. A consumed flow is not retried after provider failure or an ambiguous result; users must start sign-in again. `OAuthFlows` alone creates no application session or role grant.

HTTP login/callback routes now connect those components when an explicit shared sign-in/access service is injected. Login accepts only optional invitation UUID and `mfa=required`; completion checks the durably bound MFA requirement and original authentication age before creating a session. No client assertion can supply a role or completed-MFA flag. The callback origin must equal the configured HTTPS app origin. Cookies are host-only Secure/HttpOnly/SameSite=Lax; success replaces the browser session cookie and returns to the fixed app home, failure clears only the pending flow cookie, and app logout clears both. Domain/private API authorization remains unchanged for restricted invitees. Default unconfigured routes return 503.

Durable per-deployment fixed-minute quotas cap starts at 30 and callback attempts at 60; lower configured limits are supported. Login admission and flow creation share one conditional transaction, so a rejected/racing start cannot leave an orphan flow. Counters survive service replacement; clock rollback denies rather than resetting a quota. These development ceilings do not prove workload capacity or replace edge/concurrency protection. Flow retention cleanup and production abuse controls remain incomplete. `OAuthQueryBoundary` removes auth query strings from the original ASGI scope before routing/access logging, including malformed and unknown auth paths; a real local Uvicorn probe tests the resulting log. Hosted proxy/access-log configuration is still unverified. No actual Chrome/Cognito authentication journey was run.

`CognitoProvider` exchanges authorization codes for a public app client with PKCE, discards access/refresh tokens, fetches only the fixed issuer's public signing keys and verifies the ID token before `SignInService` can request a session. It disables environment proxies, redirects and transport retries; caps each JSON response at 64 KiB and each token at 16 KiB; and enforces per-read timeouts plus checked response deadlines. This is not a hard OS wall-clock sandbox for DNS/TLS. RS256, issuer, exact client audience, token use, nonce, verified email, strict integer times and bounded token lifetime are required. Application roles still come only from the durable membership records. Unknown, disabled or uninvited identities cannot become owners. Federation is currently rejected. Owner MFA requires the signed native password/TOTP/mfa methods and configured level-four ACR; a passkey alone or custom MFA field does not qualify. The existing access layer separately enforces recent authentication. No enrollment status is treated as proof of completing MFA.

The adapter's RSA/protocol tests are offline fixtures, not provider proof. The configured Cognito app client must allow the public authorization-code/PKCE flow; actual ACR naming, tier support, required factors, provider revocation/logout and runtime wiring remain deployment dependencies. Missing MFA claims cannot enable owner operations. The login route explicitly requests ACR/recent authentication for `mfa=required`, and completion verifies the signed result; requesting it alone is never proof that the provider honored it.

The historical host-command verifier checkpoint passed 751 tests and 56 Chrome checks (55 product scenarios plus one independent input diagnostic), all 144 synthetic development evaluations, dependency consistency, Python/frontend builds and 29 structural documentation checks. Its source-bound receipt is [local verification](../evidence/local/verification.json). The authorization-flow tests verify S256 challenge/nonce consistency, no raw secrets in persisted rows, restart persistence, configuration/key mismatch denial without consuming the original flow, exact expiry/clock rollback, expiry during commit, and one winner under simultaneous callbacks. Signed completion tests verify existing membership, rejection of an unknown/disabled identity, restricted invitation scope, no session on invalid nonce and no repeated exchange after replay/provider error. HTTP tests also cover fixed redirects and secure cookies, exact HTTPS origin, required-MFA freshness, query/body assertion rejection, no provider exchange without the bound browser cookie, failure preservation of an existing session, pending-flow cancellation on logout, durable quota reset/restart and a concurrent admission winner without orphan state. Runtime tests add the lifecycle, current-readiness, race and authorization cases described below. The real Uvicorn [redaction log](../evidence/local/signin-access-redaction.log) records paths/statuses without the synthetic callback codes/state. The synthetic provider origin is never contacted. No actual provider login, MFA, browser authentication journey or deployment was tested by this batch. Two non-failing warnings are retained: the existing Starlette test-client deprecation and the deliberately weak RSA key used by a rejection test.

## Documentation gaps and decisions reported before implementation

Worker preparation contracts now define station/runtime/profile/incarnation binding, the 160-KiB cropped-JPEG reply bound, empty-body authorized preparation, explicit lifecycle invalidation and withdrawal-only failure cleanup. `WorkerRegistry` owns real resource-limited OpenCV children and accepts one runtime, at most two live processes and 256 station fence histories. A dead/retired worker cannot restart under the old epoch; changing runtime requires a new registry. Preparation validates the server calibration JSON/hash/origin and rechecks all authority after warmup. Client readiness flags are forbidden. Worker replacement cannot race a busy analysis; invalidating an obsolete fence can interrupt its owned child and release the lease on analysis exit. Late cleanup cannot retire a newer binding. App shutdown closes its configured registry.

Actual worker output now includes a bounded JPEG of the rectified work ROI and its separate hash. Tests decode that JPEG, verify ROI dimensions and distinguish its bytes/hash from the incoming full-frame PNG. The image is excluded from repr and is not written to DynamoDB or ordinary status. The publication service stores this derivative through the private object adapter before linking evidence; the integration tests intercept S3 calls, so their receipts are not saved physical-inspection or live-cloud evidence.

`ObservationService.fail` is an internal withdrawal-only callback scoped to this runtime's admitted work. Exact pending worker/storage/provenance failure clears pending fields/current deadlines, preserves sequence/correction budgets, marks REVIEW_REQUIRED and advances the fence; reviewed closure must release the allocation. Expiry can clear pending work to INVALIDATED without resetting a healthy worker, allowing a fresh higher sequence. Old callbacks after close/new work only discard historical observations. Cleanup after user logout cannot publish or restore authority. Station close/release/takeover/profile changes also invalidate old worker bindings after the durable fence commit, preventing idle released profiles from retaining worker capacity. Tests cover actual warm preparation, repeated failure, reviewed closure, expiry recovery, logout cleanup, stale callbacks/new-worker preservation and automatic shutdown. Their metadata qualification/readiness fixtures remain synthetic and are not physical or hosted release evidence.

Capture contracts now specify pending-permit expiry/replacement, idempotent nonce derivation, original admission/permit ticks and one in-flight reservation. `CaptureService` uses the same complete atomic authority snapshot as station status, requires the originating operator session and condition-checks those rows when issuing/consuming a challenge. The nonce itself is never stored. Admission hashes the actual bounded bytes, records provenance/context/sequence and consumes exactly once; it never marks a finding verified or evidence durable. A distinct upload counter does not consume the mutating station-control allowance. Dependency deadlines and monotonic age are checked again before commit; a commit returning late reports `fresh_for_processing=false`, so future orchestration must discard/fail it rather than extend freshness. Old permit replay cannot produce a new observation or extend a deadline.

The capture component requires a server-injected warm-worker guard bound to station/epoch/runtime. Missing integration or a returned readiness boolean denies service. The actual WorkerRegistry guard and authorized preparation are now implemented; low-level protocol tests retain an explicitly synthetic guard, while separate tests exercise real warm child processes. Neither is physical camera/qualification proof. The permit route is dependency-injected and default-closed. Public observation upload remains absent until bounded upload/worker/storage/completion are connected; only the internal reservation method exists. This is not an end-to-end camera inspection workflow.

`EvidenceObjects` has no public asset endpoint. It requires an explicit account/bucket/region/KMS configuration and regional HTTPS SDK client with bounded connect/read timeouts and no automatic retries. PUT is no-overwrite and content-checksummed; a non-null version, expected encryption and pinned HEAD verification are required before returning a receipt. GET validates that version's metadata and actual byte hash/size and closes the underlying response stream. ETag is not used as a hash, and raw keys/versions are hidden from receipt repr. Format validation belongs to the bounded decoder; the byte adapter alone does not prove an image is valid or permitted. A failed/uncertain write has no receipt and needs orphan reconciliation. Tests intercept every SDK call with Stubber; no AWS storage, retention, KMS policy, byte durability, restore or release proof follows from them.

Station contracts now define owner-session acknowledgment, profile/heartbeat bodies, expected state/epoch, human disposition for unresolved work, explicit close/release reconciliation and bounded mutating-control quotas. Ownership without inspection consumes no slot. `StationService` condition-checks current actor/runtime/recipe/calibration/policy/data dependencies and atomically reserves at most two active inspection IDs; same/different-station races cannot overbook or leave orphan records. New work is only CREATED. No endpoint here accepts observations or produces PASSED_CURRENT; the conservative status path returns INVALIDATED when authority or verification is unavailable, and CLOSED history never claims a current lease.

Takeover/release/profile change close the prior work and release its reservation in the same transaction. REVIEW_REQUIRED/ACTION_REQUIRED need an explicit human disposition, which cannot become machine approval. Close preserves station ownership while advancing its fence; release clears it. Stale operation replay returns historical output plus current station metadata and cannot reacquire an obsolete epoch. Cleanup remains possible while inspection is disabled or the serving process is superseded, but still requires the owning current operator grant. Owner permission does not imply operator permission.

Allocation initialization is now part of the first internal runtime start. Later starts preserve slots, and a missing or inconsistent allocation head requires recovery rather than recreating an empty one. Runtime enable/admission condition-checks this head too. Calibration setup requires a private current dependency receipt, exact reference/purpose witnesses and matching environment/origin; beta readers reject synthetic profiles. These tests seed only synthetic private rows and prove metadata/transaction behavior, not physical calibration, blob availability, true permission or genuine release readiness. Actual assurance and media writers remain unfinished. Server-recorded heartbeat does not refresh camera evidence. Read/action/observation quotas still need the complete capture path; only mutating station controls are currently throttled.

Recipe contracts now define server-assigned IDs/revisions, `{content: ...}` create/save input, an independent catalog versus active head, exact qualification/dependency matching, bounded scoped pagination and historical approval replay. `RecipeService` writes immutable JSON revisions so numeric recipe measurements retain their canonical digest without passing binary floats through DynamoDB's numeric serializer. Draft edits preserve the active head. Approved summaries use the active revision's name and omit candidate metadata; candidate detail/listing requires owner or reviewer, and reviewer permission cannot write/approve. All writes retain current owner/MFA, Origin/CSRF, idempotency and version checks.

Approval requires a private immutable qualification record bound to exact revision/digest, artifact, environment and data epoch, plus current approved calibration and a bounded exact manifest of reference assets and purpose revisions. Assets must be committed/readable, matching origin/hash, permitted for operation and within their deadlines. Returned dependency rows must be fenced again by future inspection commits. Current reads conservatively hide approval after withdrawal, expiry, retirement or a binding change. Creation/edit/approval/retirement events include resource/revision/digest or qualification/validity details in the same transaction. No recipe API can submit passing qualification or readiness flags.

Local tests inject synthetic calibration/reference/purpose/qualification rows; these are not physical evaluation, blob existence or genuine permission evidence. Reference intake and operational-purpose writers now have local component integration; local camera enrollment/case qualification now exists; physical assurance/import, complete physical recipe qualification and runtime assurance writers remain incomplete. Source review identified missing audit detail in the initial recipe implementation, which was added before acceptance; regressions check exact journal linkage and absence of duplicate approval events. The full five-feature goal still requires those writers, station/allocation/permit orchestration and user-facing recipe/qualification journeys.

Recipe tests also verify immutable revision preservation (including fractional dimensions stored as canonical JSON), draft-versus-active names, historical replay after retirement, stale version rejection, qualification/environment/beta-origin mismatch, current dependency withdrawal/expiry, owner/reviewer/operator scope, signed cursor scope/deadline, API input/CSRF/body limits, a racing permission withdrawal and concurrent draft saves without orphan revisions. Review additionally found that raw non-ASCII cursor text could reach the HMAC comparison. Recipe and access pagination now validate bounded base64url cursor/signature syntax first; regressions require safe 400 responses without replacing the signature or scope checks.

Runtime startup and owner-enablement evidence were previously specified only at a high level. The state/API contract now defines the private incarnation-start operation, immutable receipt/review schema bindings, strict integer time fields and maximum 60-second readiness lifetime. `RuntimeControl` advances the epoch/control head and disables admission transactionally at startup. Startup replay cannot reclaim an obsolete epoch or silently change build/environment identity. Existing station, inspection and allocation records are preserved for explicit reconciliation, not reset as a side effect.

Enablement checks one current runtime, data-authority head, immutable receipt, immutable owner-review record and current owner grant in conditional transactions. Subsequent admission snapshots also check the approving owner's grant and the receipt's data epoch/deadline; callers must include those rows in their inspection commit. Receipt or review mutation, role/access changes, expiry, changed data authority and superseded runtime deny effective admission. Owner disable remains available through an old process, increments control version and clears the enable binding. Idempotent historical enable replay never turns inspection back on. A commit completing after receipt expiry returns no effective enabled state. `/health/ready` remains 503 because the full inspection/runtime dependency factory is not implemented.

Local runtime tests cover startup without owner creation, startup replay/build mismatch, concurrent startup without orphan markers, enable/disable conflicts, logout before enable commit, exact receipt expiry and clock rollback, changed data/receipt/review/owner dependencies, superseded-process disable, owner-only API authorization, CSRF/version checks and refusal of client readiness booleans. A source review also tightened malformed receipt-map rejection before calling mapping methods; regression cases cover lists, null and missing checks. No receipt type coercion is used to turn booleans/fractions/non-finite values into integer evidence.

Tests inject explicitly synthetic readiness/review rows in isolated test deployments to challenge these fences. There is no public readiness/review write endpoint, no actual assurance writer yet, and no production evidence or receipt produced by those fixtures. Actual worker/storage/configuration/recovery/release probes and their authorized review are required before genuine enablement can succeed. This component is a prerequisite for the full inspection lifecycle, not acceptance of PP06 or any Txx/E evidence.

No conflict requires replacing the chosen stack or reducing the five-feature scope. D07–D11 still lack an authorized AWS target/budget/domain, kits/operators, named support owner and finalized data arrangements. D14's physical quality/error thresholds and job/worker resource limits are not empirically established. Use explicit synthetic envelopes for development; absent required live bounds must deny the affected operation. Nothing in these local tests qualifies hardware or establishes demand/adoption.

The milestone windows must be replanned against the full 124–194-hour estimate and actual external availability. Existing documentation that describes planned behavior is a specification, not evidence that an endpoint exists. Source graph coverage is backend AST only; frontend, test and semantic document coverage are not implied. The latest incremental extraction contains 823 nodes/3347 edges with incomplete reference coverage (the initial check found 42). Treat it as incomplete navigation, not proof; current source/tests govern behavior. Extraction log is retained in `evidence/local/source-graph.log`.

## Next implementation sequence — full goal retained

1. Complete PP01 CI/ARM/supply-chain work. Tagged development fixtures, marker rectification, scene tracking and Windows resource-worker tests now exist; complete their missing physical, overlay, distortion and target-isolation cases under PP04/PP05.
2. Finish authentication runtime configuration, provider revocation/logout/provisioning, recovery, expired-flow retention and edge/concurrency protection. Login/callback routes now connect the durable flow/verifier/session components with explicit MFA, cookie/log protection and service quotas; finish their browser/provider/hosted and actor-time/network cases. The explicit programmatic runtime factory now composes the implemented services; complete its actual private host configuration, installation/service management, approved owner bootstrap and target/provider verification. Never ship a production auth bypass.
3. Finish processing/orphan recovery, read/action quotas and the remaining races/fault cases from T01–T58 on the connected multipart/permit/worker/evidence/verifier/action path; never retry approval against changed authority.
4. Complete operator part-specific overlays and interrupted-work recovery, then finish independent physical assurance/import, complete physical recipe qualification and label/replay/policy/privacy/export/exit workflows on the existing owner/reviewer workbench; test actual authenticated role journeys and accessibility. Synthetic operator browser checks do not accept these remaining paths.
5. Prepare reviewable AWS infrastructure/cost/install artifacts and tests. Only apply an exact change set after target/spend authority. Real AWS, physical and human evidence remain separate requirements; no local pass closes them.
6. Complete controlled comparisons, security/operations checks and the independently authorized qualification study when dependencies exist. Reassess all requirements and workflow child cases before any claim of completion or beta release.

Stop this build only on complete acceptance or a genuine external dependency after exhausting independent work. The current implementation is progress toward the full product, not a reduced replacement product.

## Owner, reviewer and invitation workbench

October 14, 2026: `MemberWorkbench.tsx`, `AccountsPanel.tsx`, `AuthorityPanels.tsx`, `RecipePanel.tsx` and `InvitationPanel.tsx` compose the existing authenticated APIs. A restricted invitee can inspect its bound grant/notice and explicitly accept or decline. Decline does not depend on a working notice endpoint. Acceptance rechecks the notice and clears stale acknowledgment/capability. Newly created capabilities are masked, held only in page memory and explicitly forgettable; invitation records remain NOT_SENT with no automatic delivery.

Explicit owner grants expose paginated account/invitation review, role/enabled edits, app-session revocation, invitation revocation and ownership initiation. Only the bound successor can accept a current reviewed handoff and must sign in afresh. Owner/reviewer recipe controls expose typed immutable drafts, constraints, existing qualification review, owner approval and retirement. A failed or mismatched qualification cannot enable approval. Editing clears review, and saving never implicitly activates the draft. Reviewer-only users have no owner controls or implicit operator grant.

Runtime controls show the exact current environment/epoch/control version, permit a reasoned stop, and require an existing matching unexpired private reviewed receipt plus explicit confirmation before enablement. No browser evidence writer exists. Review deadlines begin conservatively at request start; hidden/offline interruptions fence late responses. Panel navigation waits for pending work; switching from inspections stops camera tracks and preserves the same server inspection for resume. Unconfirmed sign-out hides privileged controls and offers an explicit retry. Unconfirmed structured writes retain a bounded in-memory idempotency key for an unchanged explicit retry, with no automatic retry or browser storage.

A transfer-preview regression reproduced source-owner MFA expiring before the final authorization read. `evidence/local/transfer-mfa-race-first.log` preserves the first failure; the source read now rechecks the owner role/MFA at that final boundary. A separate target-preview regression checks that expired target MFA is not presented as eligibility. The first browser run retained a fixture-name error and a keyboard-order expectation failure; both were corrected without removing assertions, and original logs/traces are kept in `evidence/local/workbench-first-*.zip` and `workbench-browser-first.log`. The later 37-check browser checkpoint passed before the final retry/accessibility additions; the current full receipt governs the final source.

All workbench browser APIs and identities are synthetic intercepted fixtures. These components do not prove provider-backed MFA, actual email delivery, complete physically qualified reference/calibration/recipe creation, label/replay/promotion, real AWS durability, privacy/export/recovery jobs, physical truth or human usability. ## Operational purpose and private reference intake

October 14, 2026: the reference request/purpose/worker contract gaps were reported before implementation and completed in STATE-DATA-API. `evidence/purposes.py` implements explicit configured-lifetime operational-use attestations, exact current notice acknowledgment, bounded scoped listing/reads and versioned withdrawal. Creation/withdrawal synchronously advance data authority. Initial internal runtime start initializes that authority once; missing history after startup requires reviewed recovery and cannot be recreated as empty state. This creates no owner and never enables inspection.

`evidence/references.py` and `evidence/http.py` authorize owner/MFA intake before body parsing, enforce a configured persistent allowance, reserve a body-bound operation/private intent, use the existing at-most-two-worker pool for reference decoding, and atomically link a verified object receipt with metadata/event/operation/data epoch. `vision/reference.py` normalizes supported single-frame orientation-normalized JPEG/PNG to metadata-free canonical JPEG inside the resource-limited child; incoming/stored hashes and dimensions remain separate. `ReferenceObjects` uses an owned `references/` namespace, and reference composition rejects the inspection-frame adapter. SDK interception verifies conditional versioned encrypted/checksummed PUT/HEAD calls; no real bucket or policy has been contacted. Reconciliation/deletion and authenticated byte retrieval remain incomplete.

The owner reference screen loads current notice/explicit lifetime limits, requires an owner source-permission attestation and chosen duration, previews a local selected image and requires explicit review before upload. The entire selected reference is transmitted, rather than claiming an inspection crop. Returned metadata must match the selected source hash, purpose, origin and deadline. Uncertain purpose creation freezes its original expiry and operation key for explicit same-body retry; uncertain image retry keeps the source and body-bound key. Successful upload/forget removes the local preview and revokes its object URL. Reviewers can read scoped metadata but have no upload, purpose-creation or withdrawal controls. Source origin remains an owner declaration and physical-truth verification remains false; neither an uploaded physical-labelled photo nor a browser checkbox can qualify a physical recipe.

Expanded local regressions cover actual child normalization/metadata removal, byte/pixel/type/orientation/protocol bounds, wrong purpose/permission/deadline, source-role/session revocation, changed data authority, corrupt/foreign storage receipt, uncertain write, exact retry, one concurrent reservation/writer, atomic link failure, expiry, purpose withdrawal, shared capacity and cancellation through pending preflight/processing/failure. The initial focused backend run passed 63 checks, and the expanded run passed 90 before the final race/namespace additions. The earlier full checkpoint passed 577 Python tests but failed one browser assertion that inspected a revocation before its response; logs/receipt are preserved as `reference-checkpoint-*`. That assertion now waits for the visible acknowledgment without removing its version check. The current source-bound receipt governs the final complete batch.

The first reference browser run also exposed a product CSP defect: default same-origin image policy blocked local blob previews. The narrow repair permits blob images only while retaining same-origin default/script/connect behavior and object/base/embedding denial. Original failure traces remain in `reference-*-csp-first-failure.zip`; all five focused reference/browser checks then passed. ## Explicit runtime composition and startup ownership

October 14, 2026: `runtime/composition.py` adds a typed programmatic factory for the implemented access, Cognito/OAuth sign-in, runtime, recipe, station, capture/publication, purpose/reference, calibration and operational-label services. They share one repository/access/runtime authority and one at-most-two-child registry. Construction is guarded by a test that rejects any SDK request at that phase. It spawns no worker, discovers no credential/profile, bootstraps no membership and enables nothing. The normal unconfigured entrypoint remains unchanged.

Configured lifespan startup proves actual local OpenCV 5, checks a fixed regional STS assumed-role/account response and verifies both distinct table identities/primary schemas before runtime state writes. Endpoints, connection/read budgets and one total SDK attempt are enforced. Beta/demo cannot use the explicit test-only loopback DynamoDB exception; local scope is marked synthetic. S3 retains its existing expected-owner/version/checksum/KMS guards. These protocol checks are not deployment/readiness, IAM-policy, bucket-policy, durability or software-attestation proof.

The factory owns successfully admitted application resources, starts each fresh server-generated instance once and leaves inspections disabled. It closes its registry/services and SDK clients on normal shutdown or startup failure, attempts remaining cleanup after a client close fails, logs no provider error text in that case and records failed status. It does not delete infrastructure or close unrelated clients. Construction failure leaves SDK ownership with the caller. A missing capture purpose supports staged authorized metadata setup but leaves publication unconfigured; configuring an actual selected purpose requires a new instance and fresh readiness rather than auto-enabling inspection.

Focused evidence: 48 composition/configuration/target/cleanup checks PASS, followed by one actual local authenticated HTTP setup check PASS using explicit trusted synthetic owner bootstrap. STS and regional table responses are intercepted; local state calls use the owned emulator; all S3 calls are intercepted. The HTTP setup creates an operational purpose without starting capture or certifying rights. An actual bounded diagnostic child is stopped by configured shutdown. Current graph extraction is 781 nodes/3150 edges, backend AST only. Use the full source-bound verifier receipt for final current results.

Assurance support uses run `runtime-composition-2026-10-14`, scope `configured-service-lifespan`, current working-tree identity from the local manifest, Change then Verify under the existing build loop. P01/P02 retain the fixed modular design and P05/P06 persona requirements; P03/P04 bind explicit target/configuration and startup completion; P05/P06 prevent identity/target expansion and automatic activation; P07 separates once-only incarnation from explicit retry with new resources; P08/P09 use intercepted target failures, actual local state/HTTP and worker cleanup; P10 preserves worker and transport budgets. No real AWS/provider/human/release evidence is inferred. Missing host invocation/bootstrap, actual target/spend/domain authority, provider recovery/revocation, readiness writers and full remaining F1-F5/operations still prevent beta acceptance.

## Retained evidence metadata, image reads and operator handoff

October 14, 2026: `evidence/reads.py` and `reads_http.py` implement scoped asset metadata, authenticated bounded app-proxied JPEG/PNG and latest-inspection asset discovery. Explicit reviewers may read permitted business evidence; operators may read their own retained inspection images; owner alone gets no blanket frame-image grant. Reference bytes require reviewer or a recent-MFA owner who originally uploaded the reference. Metadata/body checks are independent and every new request rechecks session/grant, current operational notice/attestation, source/purpose/expiry/read denial, runtime/data authority and exact private object receipt. No S3 URL/key/version or raw capture is exposed.

The service reconstructs original frame admission bindings, checks input/crop/context hashes and ordered source pointers, uses the existing version/checksum/KMS/expected-owner SDK reader, then repeats authorization/snapshot/deadline checks before delivery. Durable quota covers every authenticated attempt before resource lookup, including unknown IDs; each body read has source-bound admission/ready audits. Ready does not mean the browser received or understood bytes. At most two buffered reads run per instance, with 2 MiB maximum and explicit nonrenewable acceptance window; cancellation retains capacity until the actual blocking thread exits. These are direct history reads, not durable export/ticket jobs.

`EvidencePanel.tsx` requires explicit metadata review then image fetch, checks bound headers, bounded actual bytes and SHA-256, and creates only an ephemeral blob preview. It clears/revokes on expiry, hidden/offline state, ID changes, cancellation and navigation. It labels images historical and never a current pass. The operator's latest-evidence action stops camera capture, carries the current inspection into review and preserves the same open kit on return. Owner metadata-only access and role changes cannot be turned into image privilege by browser state. Delivered copies/screenshots cannot be recalled.

Preserved failures: `asset-reads-first.log/xml` records two test defects (wrong logout signature and awaiting the shielded task before checking capacity); their corrected tests retain the same authorization/capacity oracle. The compiler caught an unused viewer binding; the view now identifies the signed-in reader and compilation remains strict (`asset-browser-build-first.log`). `asset-read-last-expiry-first.log/xml` reproduced returning bytes with nonpositive validity after the last authentication read; final response-time validity/budget checks now deny that case. `asset-read-attempt-quota-first.log/xml` reproduced uncharged missing-ID lookups; authenticated attempts now consume quota before lookup. No deadline, role or freshness guard was weakened.

Browser history: the operator test initially omitted kit setup (`evidence-browser-expanded.log`, `evidence-operator-harness-first.zip`), then one synthetic native-canvas frame-callback timeout correctly cleared capture (`evidence-browser-final-focused.log`, `evidence-operator-frame-first.zip`). Cause of that intermittent frame timeout is unestablished. Instrumented targeted execution and three bounded repetitions subsequently passed without changing freshness limits; retain the original failure and real-camera qualification gap. Nine new evidence image scenarios and the operator handoff use intercepted APIs/canvas images only. Focused backend integration/configuration checks passed 110 tests; use the current full receipt for final current-source status. Backend AST graph is partial (823 nodes/3347 edges); frontend/transport/test source was inspected directly.

The read service is composed with shared purpose/runtime and the existing frame/reference adapters. Programmatic and host settings now require explicit `evidence_reads_per_minute`, `evidence_max_concurrent`, `evidence_read_window_ms`; existing private config is not silently granted defaults. ### Retained-read regression follow-through

The first full retained-read run passed 776 Python checks and nine local gates but failed two of 66 browser checks (`retained-read-first-verification.json`, `retained-read-first-browser-tests.log`). The keyboard assertion missed the newly added evidence navigation button; its corrected sequence retains the keyboard/zoom/contrast oracle. Scene withdrawal failed again in two of three unchanged targeted repetitions; the monitor previously added a 250 ms sampling delay and relied on a later periodic repaint. It now compares every presented fresh frame and requests repaint immediately after clearing approval. All three repeated scene checks and three keyboard checks then passed (`retained-read-browser-final.log`), with the original 500 ms withdrawal assertion and camera freshness limits unchanged. The exact physical-device latency remains unqualified; per-frame comparison overhead and noise-triggered invalidation frequency require target/physical measurements before claiming a useful operating envelope.

A separate regression reproduced returning a stored reference whose permission reference disagreed with its current purpose (`asset-read-reference-permission-first.log/xml`). Reads now require a nonempty, exact source-permission binding before delivery; mismatched, missing/null and empty bindings are negative cases. The first repaired focused asset suite passed 23 checks before expanding null/empty variants. The final complete verifier must cover the expanded sources; historical full receipts do not prove these repairs. Graph refreshed with backend AST-only coverage, 823 nodes/3347 edges; frontend/tests inspected directly. U23/I20 records the user-need implications.

The next complete run passed 779 Python checks and nine gates but failed the scene check again (65 of 66 browser checks passed); see `retained-read-second-verification.json` and `retained-read-second-browser-tests.log`. Per-frame sampling/repaint alone therefore did not establish the old timing claim. A six-run diagnostic batch preserved two failures and four passes. `retained-scene-frame-diagnostic.json` and `retained-scene-diagnostic-*.zip` record the actual requested/drawn color and sampled video pixels: one failure still displayed the old RGB [53,72,60] even though the fixture had drawn the requested RGB [180,131,89]. Another displayed the new pixels by the later diagnostic read; its exact arrival time is unknown. These observations establish a synthetic delivery confound, not a measured physical-camera latency.

The corrected scene test now first requires actual changed pixels to reach the video preview within 750 ms, then retains a 500 ms bound for approval withdrawal before the delayed result. This changes the trigger from an unpublished fixture request to an observable changed preview; it does not retrospectively pass the old request-to-withdrawal experiment. All six focused repetitions passed (`retained-scene-presented-frame.log`). Failure-only diagnostics remain in the test. The final full verification must bind this test revision; physical camera delivery/latency, burden and all human acceptance remain open.

## Separate operator human disposition and readback

October 14, 2026: inspection found the specified standalone disposition route absent and its body/readback/retry semantics underspecified. STATE-DATA-API now records the contract before implementation. StationService implements an owning-originating-operator append, immutable digest/lineage, latest readback and atomic audit/idempotency/quota checks. Corrections append; missing/corrupt prior lineage denies. Reads recheck current role/session and exact source snapshots. Role loss during commit, revocation during read, concurrent identical requests, stale/fenced/pending/closed work and owner-only writes have local negative tests. No enabled runtime or camera is required to document manual handling; no machine state, corrections, evidence, deadline or slot is changed by a human-decision append.

The operator screen stops capture before recording, requires an explicit complete manual-check declaration for human acceptance, shows historical human readback and freezes body/version/key after an uncertain response. The same request may be retried without another logical decision. Readback does not undo uncertainty; a reload/sign-out may lose the local frozen intent and requires history/supervisor review. Later close/takeover decisions are separate from the latest immutable standalone record. No human declaration establishes independent truth, machine pass or shipment authority.

The final run passes **118 checks**: 35 model/plan-signature cases, seven qualification cases, 45 existing job-authority cases, all 24 original-image replay cases, six preparation cases and the twelve-image golden test. These six complete modules ran on **519 unchanged inputs**; original failures remain preserved. Two observation-only reruns pass and export sanitized default/candidate headers, every qualification entry and all 170 software cases to [the emitted-report folder](../evidence/local/policy-qualification-emitted-reports). They overlap the 118-case run and are not extra independent trials. Actual packets show qualified synthetic_demo_only reports with 182/183 completed cases, no failures/unknowns and physical/beta/independence flags false. Provider/identity/recipe/calibration/readiness fixtures remain disclosed synthetic inputs; this is not hosted or physical acceptance.

The [scoped verification receipt](../evidence/local/policy-qualification-verification.json) binds native results, emitted packets, before/after inputs, cleanup and preserved failures. The API graph is navigation-only and DEGRADED (1418 nodes, 6402 links, 356 dangling links). No clean full-current-source verifier or new package/host proof exists. HTTP/factory/maintenance/workbench installation, consumed-qualification live validation/priority publication, full population/recovery/privacy and actual provider/target/physical/human evidence remain required. PP10 and the full goal remain incomplete; beta NO_GO.


## Consumed active-policy reader — local service verified

October 15, 2026; `PP10.consumed-policy-reader` remains a bounded child of W06/W10/W16/W18 and T19/T20/T42/T43/T48/T53. ActivePolicies reads the current POLICY row, its exact immutable activation event, genuine qualification/job and source chain, current approving-owner membership/access version, original reviewer/session authority, runtime incarnation, DATA, artifact/controls, installed registry/configuration and original expiry. Its typed snapshot carries complete bounded dependencies and a final guard. It cannot be supplied as a QualifiedPolicySnapshot for promotion. Later drafts retain the immutable active revision; a change during use invalidates the earlier snapshot. A later disable/control change does not renew qualification. Owner logout alone is distinct from revoking the approving account's grant. Actual readiness/enablement and live publication remain separate.

The private qualification grant kind was omitted from three requester/job-expiry checks. Five original probes fail before repair; all five and the 45 adjacent authority cases pass afterward. The writer's independent safeguards remain intact; no external signing exploit is claimed. The initial 19-case reader run passes, but subsequent late-authorization probes retain three DATA/configuration failures and one correctly rejected expiry. All four unchanged probes pass after moving dependency/configuration validation behind the last authorization read. Both portable repair ledgers return PARENT_REVIEW_READY for their local criteria only, parent_complete false and authority none.

Final selected verification: 220 cases across nine changed/adjacent modules and 63 preparation/evaluation/vision cases across three modules PASS without skips; independent collection reconciles all 283 identities exactly once. The 220-case cohort is repeated unchanged solely to retain its daemon diagnostic correctly. It is overlapping evidence, not 220 additional unique tests. All 524 source inputs remain unchanged through tests and packaging. Native current reports are active-policy-current-retained.xml and active-policy-compatibility.xml. Real Windows OpenCV workers and DynamoDB Local are exercised, with generated images, synthetic identities/readiness/rights and intercepted S3. The candidate source population is one; the 1000-step evaluation protocol uses disclosed injected metadata and does not establish 1000-source qualification.

An evidence audit found that earlier ad-hoc wrappers copied the wrong daemon filename. Original pytest output and cleanup receipts remain preserved, but six older daemon logs are unavailable; active-policy-daemon-capture-gap.json discloses the loss. The last compatibility daemon log was recovered before overwrite, and the unchanged 220-case rerun captures its actual daemon log. Both final logs match their cleanup hashes; owned processes terminate and their temporary state is removed. No earlier bytes are reconstructed.

The [verification receipt](../evidence/local/active-policy-verification.json) and immutable local snapshot bind current source, native tests, package bytes, repairs and limitations. Graph navigation was refreshed to 1435 nodes/6498 links but retains 360 dangling links: DEGRADED, with behavior confirmed directly in source/tests. Complete live admission/action/publication, HTTP/factory/shared-worker/workbench delivery, maximum-population qualification, full current-product verification and actual provider/physical/human acceptance remain required. All combined W/T/A acceptance stays unclosed; beta NO_GO.


Live-policy verification follow-through: two actual product regressions are retained: StationService accepted metadata-only policy admission without a trusted reader; status could return PASSED_CURRENT after completed-observation policy provenance corruption. The current guards reject both, and acknowledgment also conditions the exact completed observation. The original status probe is byte-identical to its permanent unit test; the admission function is unchanged from retained pre-operating-fixture source. The portable repair ledger validates only these two local criteria and grants no authority. Initial collection/import errors and the historical recipe DATA1/current DATA3 mismatch are test/fixture diagnostics, separately preserved. Fresh synthetic operating witnesses do not renew the historical recipe. A superseded combined evidence run timed out at its original 600-second limit with a changed source subject; its cause remains unresolved. Current whole-module evidence cohorts pass under that same per-command bound; earlier failed output is not replaced. The first installed-wheel check failed during Windows sandbox-temp pip INSTALLER rename before app execution; the workspace-temp retry installed successfully but its ASGI check timed out before startup at CPython asyncio socketpair. A standalone no-app socketpair probe reproduced the stall. The original unchanged proof passes under approved outside-sandbox local execution; no sandbox issue is declared permanently fixed. Diagnostic process enumeration found no matching live probes after their wrappers ended; failed taskkill/CIM attempts remain disclosed. Current real-action coverage still lacks measured overlap/not_flat reasons and complete specific overlays; a missing-part action does not establish changed-policy benefit.

## Original/crop metadata validation — retained failures and scope

The private exact-boolean metadata_only mode defaults false. It validates retained committed/denied/deleted original/crop metadata, exact stored keys and bounded revisions, denial job/time and coherent version-check progress while retaining completed source/context/purpose/origin/calibration/format/retention checks. Ordinary delivery checks remain unchanged. Missing/erased/corrupt metadata holds; no public flag, object read, cleanup plan, read permission, deletion or clock renewal follows. The complete family adapter remains required.

The pre-feature product oracle fails at the absent keyword. The initial test invocation used the wrong import root, and its next fixture incorrectly passed a string into a strict Finding enum; both precede and are distinct from the valid product baseline. A first package attempt correctly fails because the isolated tree lacked the required built frontend; copying the exact unchanged three-file artifact enables the separately named passing build. A first archived-oracle rerun selects the older canonical module via parent pytest configuration and fails nine cases; the corrected probe explicitly verifies the staged module path and uses the stage config, passing the same literal nine cases without changing that oracle. All original outputs, snapshot hashes and the pending-parent repair history remain under `.loop/repairs/metadata-original-metadata`. Its component ledger is PARENT_REVIEW_READY, not full-goal acceptance.
